Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I know you're very busy, so I really appreciate it. Um, I'm just here because... well, I guess what finally made me make the appointment is that my appetite has been a little... all over the place lately. For a few months now, really. 

It's nothing terrible, I promise. Just... there are stretches where I end up skipping dinner entirely, and I've lost a little weight without really trying to. And then other nights, I just sort of pick at crackers or have a bowl of cereal because... well, cooking just for myself feels a bit pointless sometimes. I hope that makes sense? I just thought I should get it checked out to be safe.

I think it's just because, like I was saying earlier, my appetite has been so up and down for the past few months. I'll have these stretches where I just end up skipping dinner entirely, and I suppose the weight just sort of slips off without me trying. And then, you know, other nights I just grab a handful of crackers or some cereal because cooking a whole meal just for myself seems a bit silly. 

So yes, my clothes are a little looser, but it's nothing drastic, I promise. I'm sure it's just a phase, right? I just wanted to run it by you to make sure I'm doing the right thing by checking in.

Oh, um... guilty? Well, I wouldn't say I feel guilty about everything, exactly. I mean, I try really hard to do my best at work and be there for people, so I don't think I'm doing a bad job overall.

Oh, um... well, I guess since you asked it that way... I suppose there has been a bit of a decrease. I didn't want to make a big fuss about it before, because I really am still trying!

Oh, um... well, I guess it's a lot like what I was just mentioning about the garden and the book club. I really wouldn't want to say I have zero interest, because that sounds so awful, doesn't it? I don't want to seem like I've just given up.

Oh, gosh... "traumatic" feels like such a strong word. I mean, I know you mentioned the marriage ending, and yes, that was... well, it was a very difficult adjustment, certainly. But I really wouldn't want to call it a trauma.

I just think maybe I'm a little tired from work. Does that sound normal to you? I really hope I'm not wasting your time with all this.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, like I mentioned when I first came in, I have lost a little bit of weight, yes. But I wouldn't call it \"significant,\" really! \n\nI think it's just because, like I was saying earlier, my appetite has been so up and down for the past few months. I'll have these stretches where I just end up skipping dinner entirely, and I suppose the weight just sort of slips off without me trying. And then, you know, other nights I just grab a handful of crackers or some cereal because cooking a whole meal just for myself seems a bit silly. \n\nSo yes, my clothes are a little looser, but it's nothing drastic, I promise. I'm sure it's just a phase, right? I just wanted to run it by you to make sure I'm doing the right thing by checking in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... guilty? Well, I wouldn't say I feel guilty about everything, exactly. I mean, I try really hard to do my best at work and be there for people, so I don't think I'm doing a bad job overall. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest, I do have these moments where I get a little stuck in my head about my marriage ending. It's silly, I know, but I just keep replaying things and telling myself I must have failed at something really basic for it to fall apart like that. I mean, logically, part of me knows relationships are much more complicated than just one person doing something wrong, right? You see that all the time in my line of work. \n\nBut I still just catch myself thinking I should have been better somehow. I try not to dwell on it too much, though! I know it's not productive. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not oversharing or making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... anxious? No, not really. I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I feel on edge or anything like that. \n\nI think I'm pretty calm, for the most part. Of course, my job can be a little stressful sometimes, just making sure all my clients are getting what they need, but I handle it fine. I don't sit around worrying or feeling jittery or anything. I just try to take things one day at a time, you know? \n\nSo no, I don't think anxiety is really a problem for me. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say I'm not enjoying things at all. I mean, I still try to keep up with my hobbies! I know how important it is to stay active. \n\nI still go out and water the garden, and I make sure to show up to my book club meetings. I don't want to let anyone down, you know? But, I suppose... if I'm really thinking about it, lately it just feels a little... flat? Like I'm just going through the motions. Sometimes I'll get out to the garden and just sort of stare at it without really starting, or I'll end up leaving book club a little early. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, I promise! I just think maybe I'm a little tired lately. But I am still trying my best to stay engaged. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in how much you enjoy the things you're still doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess since you asked it that way... I suppose there has been a bit of a decrease. I didn't want to make a big fuss about it before, because I really am still trying! \n\nBut yes, if I'm being completely honest, when I'm out there with the watering can, or sitting with the girls at book club... it just doesn't have that same spark it used to. It's like I said, it just feels very flat. I'll get all set up to garden and then just kind of stand there, or I'll make an excuse to slip out of the discussion early because I just can't seem to get into it. \n\nI feel awful admitting that, because I know I should be grateful for my hobbies and my friends. It's just harder to find the joy in it right now, even though I'm going through all the right motions. Does that make sense? I know that sounds like a long time to still be feeling this way, and I really should be over it by now, shouldn't I? I just really don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any decrease in interest in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But it's really nothing major, I promise. \n\nI still do the things I usually enjoy. Like, I still go out and water the garden, and I make sure to show up for my book club. I wouldn't want to let anyone down or seem like I'm flaking out, you know? It's just... lately, when I'm doing those things, it feels a bit... flat? I don't know if that's the right word. Sometimes I end up leaving book club a little early, or I just kind of stare at the garden instead of actually getting started on the weeding. \n\nBut I'm still keeping up with everything, for the most part! I just think maybe I'm a little tired from work. Does that sound normal to you? I really hope I'm not wasting your time with all this.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, like I mentioned when I first came in, I have lost a little bit of weight, yes. But I wouldn't call it \"significant,\" really! \n\nI think it's just because, like I was saying earlier, my appetite has been so up and down for the past few months. I'll have these stretches where I just end up skipping dinner entirely, and I suppose the weight just sort of slips off without me trying. And then, you know, other nights I just grab a handful of crackers or some cereal because cooking a whole meal just for myself seems a bit silly. \n\nSo yes, my clothes are a little looser, but it's nothing drastic, I promise. I'm sure it's just a phase, right? I just wanted to run it by you to make sure I'm doing the right thing by checking in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... guilty? Well, I wouldn't say I feel guilty about everything, exactly. I mean, I try really hard to do my best at work and be there for people, so I don't think I'm doing a bad job overall. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest, I do have these moments where I get a little stuck in my head about my marriage ending. It's silly, I know, but I just keep replaying things and telling myself I must have failed at something really basic for it to fall apart like that. I mean, logically, part of me knows relationships are much more complicated than just one person doing something wrong, right? You see that all the time in my line of work. \n\nBut I still just catch myself thinking I should have been better somehow. I try not to dwell on it too much, though! I know it's not productive. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not oversharing or making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... anxious? No, not really. I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I feel on edge or anything like that. \n\nI think I'm pretty calm, for the most part. Of course, my job can be a little stressful sometimes, just making sure all my clients are getting what they need, but I handle it fine. I don't sit around worrying or feeling jittery or anything. I just try to take things one day at a time, you know? \n\nSo no, I don't think anxiety is really a problem for me. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say I'm not enjoying things at all. I mean, I still try to keep up with my hobbies! I know how important it is to stay active. \n\nI still go out and water the garden, and I make sure to show up to my book club meetings. I don't want to let anyone down, you know? But, I suppose... if I'm really thinking about it, lately it just feels a little... flat? Like I'm just going through the motions. Sometimes I'll get out to the garden and just sort of stare at it without really starting, or I'll end up leaving book club a little early. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, I promise! I just think maybe I'm a little tired lately. But I am still trying my best to stay engaged. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in how much you enjoy the things you're still doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess since you asked it that way... I suppose there has been a bit of a decrease. I didn't want to make a big fuss about it before, because I really am still trying! \n\nBut yes, if I'm being completely honest, when I'm out there with the watering can, or sitting with the girls at book club... it just doesn't have that same spark it used to. It's like I said, it just feels very flat. I'll get all set up to garden and then just kind of stand there, or I'll make an excuse to slip out of the discussion early because I just can't seem to get into it. \n\nI feel awful admitting that, because I know I should be grateful for my hobbies and my friends. It's just harder to find the joy in it right now, even though I'm going through all the right motions. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding ungrateful or anything.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like things are flat and you're just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... let me think. I guess... well, I really didn't want to bring this up, because I don't want to sound like I'm just dwelling on things, you know? I really have been trying to move forward. \n\nBut I suppose it started around the time my divorce was finalized. So that would be... gosh, about a year and seven months ago now. I know that sounds like a long time to still be feeling this way, and I really should be over it by now, shouldn't I? Other than the divorce—which I really am trying my very best to handle gracefully—no, I haven't been through anything like that. I really don't have anything else to report. \n\nI hope it's alright that I say that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you accurate information and not making a mountain out of a molehill. I know how important self-care is—I tell my own clients that all the time! ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, yes, definitely more than six months. I know I keep bringing it back to the same timeline, and I really hope you don't mind me repeating it again. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the most accurate picture possible. 

But yes, it's been going on since the divorce was finalized... so that would be about a year and seven months ago now. I really thought I would have shaken it off by now, you know? I keep telling myself I should be doing better, and I feel a bit silly that it's dragged on this long. I hope it's okay that I haven't been able to just snap out of it yet. I really am trying!

And then other nights, I just sort of pick at crackers or have a bowl of cereal because... well, cooking just for myself feels a bit pointless sometimes. I hope that makes sense? I just thought I should get it checked out to be safe.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... sad? No, I wouldn't say that. I haven't really noticed feeling down, exactly. \n\nI mean, being a social worker, I see people every day who are truly going through so much, and I definitely wouldn't want to complain or, you know, compare my situation to theirs. It's silly, I know, but I just keep replaying things and telling myself I must have failed at something really basic for it to fall apart like that. I mean, logically, part of me knows relationships are much more complicated than just one person doing something wrong, right? Does that make sense? I hope I'm not oversharing or making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... anxious? No, not really. I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I feel on edge or anything like that. \n\nI think I'm pretty calm, for the most part. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say I'm not enjoying things at all. I don't want to let anyone down, you know? But, I suppose... if I'm really thinking about it, lately it just feels a little... flat? Like I'm just going through the motions. Sometimes I'll get out to the garden and just sort of stare at it without really starting, or I'll end up leaving book club a little early. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, I promise! I just think maybe I'm a little tired lately. But I am still trying my best to stay engaged. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in how much you enjoy the things you're still doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess since you asked it that way... I suppose there has been a bit of a decrease. I didn't want to make a big fuss about it before, because I really am still trying! \n\nBut yes, if I'm being completely honest, when I'm out there with the watering can, or sitting with the girls at book club... it just doesn't have that same spark it used to. It's like I said, it just feels very flat. I'll get all set up to garden and then just kind of stand there, or I'll make an excuse to slip out of the discussion early because I just can't seem to get into it. \n\nI feel awful admitting that, because I know I should be grateful for my hobbies and my friends. It's just harder to find the joy in it right now, even though I'm going through all the right motions. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding ungrateful or anything.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like things are flat and you're just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... let me think. I really hope I'm not wasting your time with all this.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, like I mentioned when I first came in, I have lost a little bit of weight, yes. But I wouldn't call it \"significant,\" really! \n\nI think it's just because, like I was saying earlier, my appetite has been so up and down for the past few months. I'll have these stretches where I just end up skipping dinner entirely, and I suppose the weight just sort of slips off without me trying. And then, you know, other nights I just grab a handful of crackers or some cereal because cooking a whole meal just for myself seems a bit silly. \n\nSo yes, my clothes are a little looser, but it's nothing drastic, I promise. I'm sure it's just a phase, right? I just wanted to run it by you to make sure I'm doing the right thing by checking in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... guilty? Well, I wouldn't say I feel guilty about everything, exactly. I mean, I try really hard to do my best at work and be there for people, so I don't think I'm doing a bad job overall. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest, I do have these moments where I get a little stuck in my head about my marriage ending. It's silly, I know, but I just keep replaying things and telling myself I must have failed at something really basic for it to fall apart like that. I mean, logically, part of me knows relationships are much more complicated than just one person doing something wrong, right? You see that all the time in my line of work. \n\nBut I still just catch myself thinking I should have been better somehow. I try not to dwell on it too much, though! I know it's not productive. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not oversharing or making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... anxious? No, not really. I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I feel on edge or anything like that. \n\nI think I'm pretty calm, for the most part. Of course, my job can be a little stressful sometimes, just making sure all my clients are getting what they need, but I handle it fine. I don't sit around worrying or feeling jittery or anything. I just try to take things one day at a time, you know? \n\nSo no, I don't think anxiety is really a problem for me. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say I'm not enjoying things at all. I mean, I still try to keep up with my hobbies! I know how important it is to stay active. \n\nI still go out and water the garden, and I make sure to show up to my book club meetings. I don't want to let anyone down, you know? But, I suppose... if I'm really thinking about it, lately it just feels a little... flat? Like I'm just going through the motions. Sometimes I'll get out to the garden and just sort of stare at it without really starting, or I'll end up leaving book club a little early. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, I promise! I just think maybe I'm a little tired lately. But I am still trying my best to stay engaged. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in how much you enjoy the things you're still doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess since you asked it that way... I suppose there has been a bit of a decrease. I didn't want to make a big fuss about it before, because I really am still trying! \n\nBut yes, if I'm being completely honest, when I'm out there with the watering can, or sitting with the girls at book club... it just doesn't have that same spark it used to. It's like I said, it just feels very flat. I'll get all set up to garden and then just kind of stand there, or I'll make an excuse to slip out of the discussion early because I just can't seem to get into it. \n\nI feel awful admitting that, because I know I should be grateful for my hobbies and my friends. It's just harder to find the joy in it right now, even though I'm going through all the right motions. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding ungrateful or anything.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like things are flat and you're just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... let me think. I guess... well, I really didn't want to bring this up, because I don't want to sound like I'm just dwelling on things, you know? I really have been trying to move forward. \n\nBut I suppose it started around the time my divorce was finalized. So that would be... gosh, about a year and seven months ago now. I know that sounds like a long time to still be feeling this way, and I really should be over it by now, shouldn't I? I try so hard to keep a positive attitude at work with my clients, but I guess it just took a bit of a toll on me personally. \n\nI really hope it's okay that I shared that. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right. Am I giving you the right kind of timeline?\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are activities that you used to find very enjoyable, but now you have little to no interest in doing them at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a lot like what I was just mentioning about the garden and the book club. I really wouldn't want to say I have zero interest, because that sounds so awful, doesn't it? I don't want to seem like I've just given up. \n\nBut... yes, I suppose if I'm looking at it honestly, I really am just going through the motions with those things I used to absolutely love. Most of the time it just feels so flat. Like I said, I'll get all my gardening tools out and then just... not even start. Or I'll go to see the girls and just quietly excuse myself early because I can't muster up the interest to participate. \n\nI feel so guilty admitting that, because I know I should be doing better. I really am trying to keep up with it all. Does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in the past, such as the end of your marriage, that might be contributing to your current feelings?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... \"traumatic\" feels like such a strong word. I mean, I know you mentioned the marriage ending, and yes, that was... well, it was a very difficult adjustment, certainly. But I really wouldn't want to call it a trauma. \n\nIn my line of work, I see clients who have been through *actual* traumatic events, so I'd feel just terrible claiming that word for myself when I've honestly had a pretty fortunate life. Other than the divorce—which I really am trying my very best to handle gracefully—no, I haven't been through anything like that. I really don't have anything else to report. \n\nI hope it's alright that I say that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you accurate information and not making a mountain out of a molehill. I'd hate for you to think I'm exaggerating things!\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're just going through the motions with your hobbies and activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose it's exactly like I mentioned a little bit ago. I hope you don't mind me repeating myself! I just want to make sure I'm being accurate for you. But, yes, the trouble with my hobbies... it really started right when the divorce was finalized. So it's been about a year and seven months for that, too. \n\nI really thought I would have bounced back and gotten back into my garden and my reading by now. I know how important self-care is—I tell my own clients that all the time! But I guess the feeling has just sort of lingered since then, despite my best efforts to push through it. \n\nIs it okay that it's the exact same timeframe? I just want to be as helpful as I can with your questions.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have lost most of the interest you used to have in the activities you still participate in?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, yes... I know we sort of touched on this just a moment ago, so I hope I'm not being too repetitive! I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking. \n\nBut, yes, I suppose if I'm being completely honest, I have lost most of that spark. I really do try to keep up appearances, you know? I still go through the motions—I'll go out to water the garden, or I'll show up to the book club because I don't want to let the girls down. But... it just feels so flat. Half the time I end up leaving early, or I'll get everything ready to garden and then just... not even start. \n\nI feel terrible saying that, because I know I should be more engaged. Oh, um... I wouldn't really say I have periods where I feel completely back to normal, no. I hope that's not disappointing to hear. I know I should probably be having some good days where I just bounce back, but... it's more like a constant, low-level hum, I guess?

Oh, um... traumatic? I don't... I don't know if I would use that word, exactly. I mean, it was definitely sad, and a big adjustment, but... traumatic sounds so dramatic, doesn't it? I wouldn't want to make it seem like I'm blowing things out of proportion or, you know, playing the victim.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F43.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I know you're very busy, so I really appreciate it. Um, I'm just here because... well, I guess what finally made me make the appointment is that my appetite has been a little... all over the place lately. For a few months now, really. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, I promise. Just... there are stretches where I end up skipping dinner entirely, and I've lost a little weight without really trying to. And then other nights, I just sort of pick at crackers or have a bowl of cereal because... well, cooking just for myself feels a bit pointless sometimes. I hope that makes sense? I just thought I should get it checked out to be safe.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... sad? No, I wouldn't say that. I haven't really noticed feeling down, exactly. \n\nI mean, being a social worker, I see people every day who are truly going through so much, and I definitely wouldn't want to complain or, you know, compare my situation to theirs. I think I'm doing okay emotionally? It's really just the eating thing I mentioned. I don't think I've been sad... I hope that's helpful? I just really don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any decrease in interest in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But it's really nothing major, I promise. \n\nI still do the things I usually enjoy. Like, I still go out and water the garden, and I make sure to show up for my book club. I wouldn't want to let anyone down or seem like I'm flaking out, you know? It's just... lately, when I'm doing those things, it feels a bit... flat? I don't know if that's the right word. Sometimes I end up leaving book club a little early, or I just kind of stare at the garden instead of actually getting started on the weeding. \n\nBut I'm still keeping up with everything, for the most part! I just think maybe I'm a little tired from work. Does that sound normal to you? I really hope I'm not wasting your time with all this.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, like I mentioned when I first came in, I have lost a little bit of weight, yes. But I wouldn't call it \"significant,\" really! \n\nI think it's just because, like I was saying earlier, my appetite has been so up and down for the past few months. I'll have these stretches where I just end up skipping dinner entirely, and I suppose the weight just sort of slips off without me trying. And then, you know, other nights I just grab a handful of crackers or some cereal because cooking a whole meal just for myself seems a bit silly. \n\nSo yes, my clothes are a little looser, but it's nothing drastic, I promise. I'm sure it's just a phase, right? I just wanted to run it by you to make sure I'm doing the right thing by checking in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... guilty? Well, I wouldn't say I feel guilty about everything, exactly. I mean, I try really hard to do my best at work and be there for people, so I don't think I'm doing a bad job overall. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest, I do have these moments where I get a little stuck in my head about my marriage ending. It's silly, I know, but I just keep replaying things and telling myself I must have failed at something really basic for it to fall apart like that. I mean, logically, part of me knows relationships are much more complicated than just one person doing something wrong, right? You see that all the time in my line of work. \n\nBut I still just catch myself thinking I should have been better somehow. I try not to dwell on it too much, though! I know it's not productive. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not oversharing or making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... anxious? No, not really. I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I feel on edge or anything like that. \n\nI think I'm pretty calm, for the most part. Of course, my job can be a little stressful sometimes, just making sure all my clients are getting what they need, but I handle it fine. I don't sit around worrying or feeling jittery or anything. I just try to take things one day at a time, you know? \n\nSo no, I don't think anxiety is really a problem for me. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say I'm not enjoying things at all. I mean, I still try to keep up with my hobbies! I know how important it is to stay active. \n\nI still go out and water the garden, and I make sure to show up to my book club meetings. I don't want to let anyone down, you know? But, I suppose... if I'm really thinking about it, lately it just feels a little... flat? Like I'm just going through the motions. Sometimes I'll get out to the garden and just sort of stare at it without really starting, or I'll end up leaving book club a little early. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, I promise! I just think maybe I'm a little tired lately. But I am still trying my best to stay engaged. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in how much you enjoy the things you're still doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess since you asked it that way... I suppose there has been a bit of a decrease. I didn't want to make a big fuss about it before, because I really am still trying! \n\nBut yes, if I'm being completely honest, when I'm out there with the watering can, or sitting with the girls at book club... it just doesn't have that same spark it used to. It's like I said, it just feels very flat. I'll get all set up to garden and then just kind of stand there, or I'll make an excuse to slip out of the discussion early because I just can't seem to get into it. \n\nI feel awful admitting that, because I know I should be grateful for my hobbies and my friends. It's just harder to find the joy in it right now, even though I'm going through all the right motions. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding ungrateful or anything.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like things are flat and you're just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... let me think. I guess... well, I really didn't want to bring this up, because I don't want to sound like I'm just dwelling on things, you know? I really have been trying to move forward. \n\nBut I suppose it started around the time my divorce was finalized. So that would be... gosh, about a year and seven months ago now. I know that sounds like a long time to still be feeling this way, and I really should be over it by now, shouldn't I? I try so hard to keep a positive attitude at work with my clients, but I guess it just took a bit of a toll on me personally. \n\nI really hope it's okay that I shared that. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right. Am I giving you the right kind of timeline?\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are activities that you used to find very enjoyable, but now you have little to no interest in doing them at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a lot like what I was just mentioning about the garden and the book club. I really wouldn't want to say I have zero interest, because that sounds so awful, doesn't it? I don't want to seem like I've just given up. \n\nBut... yes, I suppose if I'm looking at it honestly, I really am just going through the motions with those things I used to absolutely love. Most of the time it just feels so flat. Like I said, I'll get all my gardening tools out and then just... not even start. Or I'll go to see the girls and just quietly excuse myself early because I can't muster up the interest to participate. \n\nI feel so guilty admitting that, because I know I should be doing better. I really am trying to keep up with it all. Does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in the past, such as the end of your marriage, that might be contributing to your current feelings?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... \"traumatic\" feels like such a strong word. I mean, I know you mentioned the marriage ending, and yes, that was... well, it was a very difficult adjustment, certainly. But I really wouldn't want to call it a trauma. \n\nIn my line of work, I see clients who have been through *actual* traumatic events, so I'd feel just terrible claiming that word for myself when I've honestly had a pretty fortunate life. Other than the divorce—which I really am trying my very best to handle gracefully—no, I haven't been through anything like that. I really don't have anything else to report. \n\nI hope it's alright that I say that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you accurate information and not making a mountain out of a molehill. I'd hate for you to think I'm exaggerating things!\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're just going through the motions with your hobbies and activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose it's exactly like I mentioned a little bit ago. I hope you don't mind me repeating myself! I just want to make sure I'm being accurate for you. But, yes, the trouble with my hobbies... it really started right when the divorce was finalized. So it's been about a year and seven months for that, too. \n\nI really thought I would have bounced back and gotten back into my garden and my reading by now. I know how important self-care is—I tell my own clients that all the time! But I guess the feeling has just sort of lingered since then, despite my best efforts to push through it. \n\nIs it okay that it's the exact same timeframe? I just want to be as helpful as I can with your questions.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have lost most of the interest you used to have in the activities you still participate in?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, yes... I know we sort of touched on this just a moment ago, so I hope I'm not being too repetitive! I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking. \n\nBut, yes, I suppose if I'm being completely honest, I have lost most of that spark. I really do try to keep up appearances, you know? I still go through the motions—I'll go out to water the garden, or I'll show up to the book club because I don't want to let the girls down. But... it just feels so flat. Half the time I end up leaving early, or I'll get everything ready to garden and then just... not even start. \n\nI feel terrible saying that, because I know I should be more engaged. It sounds awful, doesn't it? Like I'm not even trying. But I really am trying my best to participate. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than six months?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, definitely more than six months. I know I keep bringing it back to the same timeline, and I really hope you don't mind me repeating it again. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the most accurate picture possible. \n\nBut yes, it's been going on since the divorce was finalized... so that would be about a year and seven months ago now. I really thought I would have shaken it off by now, you know? I keep telling myself I should be doing better, and I feel a bit silly that it's dragged on this long. I hope it's okay that I haven't been able to just snap out of it yet. I really am trying!\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are things you used to enjoy that you now find you have almost no interest in doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, yes... I think that's exactly it. I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record bringing up the gardening and the book club again! I really hope you don't mind me repeating myself. \n\nBut yes, it's just like I was saying... I still try to go through the motions with those things I used to absolutely love. I really do try to push myself to do them because I don't want to just give up. But... honestly, the interest just isn't there anymore. It all just feels so flat. Like I mentioned, I'll get the hose out to water the plants and then I just... I can't even bring myself to start. Or I'll go to book club and just quietly slip out early because I'm just not engaged at all. \n\nI know I should be trying harder to actually enjoy them, and I feel a bit guilty that I can't right now. Is it okay that my answer is basically the same as before? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your feelings of going through the motions and lack of interest in activities are directly related to your divorce?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose that makes sense, doesn't it? I mean, the timing certainly lines up, since it all really started feeling this way after everything was finalized. I know I keep bringing the divorce up, and I hope I'm not making it sound like I'm just wallowing in it or... or blaming everything on my ex-husband. I really don't want to come across as bitter! \n\nI guess I'm not entirely sure if it's *directly* related, or if it's just... a coincidence? I mean, a lot changed all at once, so maybe it's just the stress of adjusting to everything. I really try not to dwell on it too much. Do you think that's what's causing it? I hope I'm not answering this wrong, I just... I haven't really thought about it as a direct cause-and-effect kind of thing. I just know I haven't quite been myself. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're back to your normal self between periods of feeling flat or going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I wouldn't really say I have periods where I feel completely back to normal, no. I hope that's not disappointing to hear. I know I should probably be having some good days where I just bounce back, but... it's more like a constant, low-level hum, I guess? \n\nI mean, I definitely have days where I can push through better than others, especially at work. Being a social worker, I really have to be present for my clients, and I try so hard to make sure I'm giving them my best self. So I can sort of... put on a good face and get things done. But even then, underneath it all, I still feel that same flatness we talked about. \n\nIt never really goes away completely. It just sort of... hovers. I feel a bit bad admitting that, like I'm just not trying hard enough to find the joy in the good moments. I really hope I'm explaining this clearly enough for you? Is it normal for it to just stay kind of steady like that, without really breaking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the end of your marriage was traumatic for you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... traumatic? I don't... I don't know if I would use that word, exactly. I mean, it was definitely sad, and a big adjustment, but... traumatic sounds so dramatic, doesn't it? I wouldn't want to make it seem like I'm blowing things out of proportion or, you know, playing the victim. \n\nI mean, people go through much worse things all the time, right? Especially in my line of work, I see people dealing with real trauma every day. So to call my divorce traumatic feels a bit... disrespectful to them, almost? \n\nIt was just a difficult life transition. We just grew apart, and it was hard to let go of the life we built, but... I don't think it was trauma. I hope I'm not downplaying it if you think it's important, but... does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture without sounding like I'm overreacting.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
